    *,
    state_snapshot: object | None = None,
) -> AstridError:
    """Wrap an escaped bare exception as a degraded Astrid envelope."""

    snapshot = build_state_snapshot(
        state_snapshot,
        original_type=type(value).__name__,
    )
    return coerce_astrid_error(value, state_snapshot=snapshot, degraded=True)


def render_astrid_error(error: AstridError) -> int:
    """Render an ``AstridError`` envelope to stderr and return its exit code."""

    if error.degraded:
        print("unstructured - this is a bug.", file=sys.stderr)
    print(error.cause, file=sys.stderr)
    if error.valid_options:
        print(f"valid options: {', '.join(error.valid_options)}", file=sys.stderr)
    if error.recovery_command:
        print(f"recovery: {error.recovery_command}", file=sys.stderr)
    if error.state_snapshot:
        print(
            f"state snapshot: {json.dumps(error.state_snapshot, sort_keys=True)}",
            file=sys.stderr,
        )
    return 1 if error.degraded else 2


def _first_text_attr(value: object, *names: str) -> str | None:
    for name in names:
        candidate = getattr(value, name, None)
        if isinstance(candidate, str) and candidate:
            return candidate
    return None
"""Shared subprocess runner for orchestrator steps.

Minimal helper that wraps :func:`subprocess.run` with consistent
:class:`AstridError` raising on non-zero exit.  All orchestrators that
shell out to child commands should route through this module so
recovery_command and state_snapshot metadata stay uniform.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import subprocess
from typing import Any

from astrid.core.contracts.errors import AstridError


def run_subprocess(
    cmd: list[str],
    *,
    label: str,
    orchestrator: str | None = None,
    **kwargs: Any,
) -> str:
    """Run *cmd* and return its captured stdout.

    ``capture_output=True`` and ``text=True`` are hard-coded.  Extra
    keyword arguments (e.g. ``env``, ``cwd``) are forwarded to
    :func:`subprocess.run`.

    Raises :class:`AstridError` when the child exits non-zero.
    """
    proc = subprocess.run(cmd, capture_output=True, text=True, **kwargs)

    if proc.returncode != 0:
        prefix = f"[{orchestrator}] " if orchestrator else ""
        raise AstridError(
            f"{prefix}{label} failed (exit {proc.returncode})",
            recovery_command=(
                f"check the child command and rerun: {' '.join(cmd)}"
            ),
            state_snapshot={
                "command": " ".join(cmd),
                "stdout": proc.stdout,
                "stderr": proc.stderr,
            },
        )

    return proc.stdout


__all__ = ["run_subprocess"]

codex
# 1. Entire-project tasklist

No files were changed during this planning pass. The 18 locked decisions remain unchanged. The following closes all ten open questions and decomposes M1 and M2 into independently reviewable, test-gated units.

## Resolved architecture decisions

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| 1 | Use a rendering-specific pack extension, backed by the existing pack kernel—not ordinary child executors. `pack.yaml` gains an optional `extensions.rendering` section containing pack-relative `renderer.yaml`, planner, and finalizer manifests. Reuse `discover_pack_metadata`, `CapabilityRegistry`, `AliasResolver`, and `OverrideStore`; do not create another pack loader. |
| 2 | `rendering.render` owns the sole executor/project run. It calls backend commands directly through the render host, so backends do not create nested runs, ledgers, task gates, or CLI recursion. External callers invoke the `rendering.render` capability; only its facade calls the internal `RenderService`. |
| 3 | Minimal renderer metadata is: qualified `id`, manifest/protocol version, argv `command`, supported operations, coarse `clip_types`, `track_types`, feature tokens, full-timeline/window support, output-profile hints, required binaries, and the subset of pack permissions it needs. Missing capabilities fail closed. Optional `support` is the authoritative request-sensitive answer. |
| 4 | The canonical request carries `backend_config: { "<qualified-id>": {...} }`. Legacy typed executor flags may translate into that map at the facade, but backend-specific fields never enter the core request schema. Only the selected backend receives its map. |
| 5 | Provenance v2 keeps top-level compatibility projections for `schema_version`, `engine`, `output`, `timeline`, and `assets_registry`. `engine` records the legacy request; new requested/resolved fields are authoritative. Remotion-only fields move under a backend-owned `rendering.remotion` fragment. Deprecated v1 Remotion and segment fields are mirrored for one compatibility window, then removed only after a consumer audit. |
| 6 | `ffmpeg` maps to strict `rendering.ffmpeg`; `remotion` retains the current legacy policy—FFmpeg for eligible media/audio-specialized timelines, otherwise `rendering.remotion`; `hybrid` maps to `rendering.legacy_hybrid`, never a renderer ID. Qualified `rendering.remotion` and `rendering.ffmpeg` are strict. An auto-routing warning plus provenance reason makes legacy `remotion` behavior visible; a later release can move it to `planner=auto`. |
| 7 | The current Sprint 08 “parity” fixture is not authoritative: it contains no render fixtures and hashes input JSON. Gate extraction on semantic command/props/provenance and `ffprobe` parity using the existing Remotion registry/theme tests, audio-reactive fixture, Hype merged-props golden, and `reshape/hype_regression`; add committed 2-second fixtures. Do not require byte-identical MP4s. |
| 8 | The Python author API is functional, without inheritance: `render(request, context)` plus optional `support(request, context)`, registered through an SDK `renderer_main(...)` helper. `RenderContext` supplies resolved assets, allocated paths, declared-permission checks, subprocess/log/progress helpers, read-only synchronous interruption state, probing, hashes, audio completion, attachments, and cleanup. The wire protocol—not the SDK—defines semantics. |
| 9 | Every asset uses one descriptor with original reference, hash/metadata, and whichever of `local_path`, invocation-scoped `local_url`, or existing `remote_url` are available. The host materializes local/cache copies and starts Range serving only when requested. Remote adapters upload themselves under declared permissions; V1 adds no general remote-upload service. |
| 10 | The public author surface is `astrid renderers create/list/inspect/validate/smoke/replay`. `list` and `inspect` are static. `validate` is static unless explicit conformance execution is requested; `smoke` and `replay` execute code. All return a common JSON envelope: `schema_version`, `command`, `ok`, `data`, and `errors`, with backend-qualified symbolic errors and `recovery_command`. Expected failures retain Astrid’s exit 2 convention; escaped bugs return 1; interruption returns 130. Pack installation remains under `astrid packs`. A thin kind-aware override administration path should reuse `OverrideStore`, not copy executor override logic. |

The concrete extension shape to freeze in M1 is:

```yaml
extensions:
  rendering:
    renderers:
      - renderer.yaml
    planners: []
    finalizers: []
```

Manifest paths are pack-relative, must remain inside the pack, and are parsed statically. Commands run with `shell=False`, with the pack root as `cwd`. The V1 invocation shape is:

```text
<manifest command...> render|support|plan|finalize \
  --request <absolute-request.json> \
  --result <absolute-result.json>
```

The result file is authoritative; stdout and stderr are captured as logs.

## M1 — Renderer kernel and built-ins

- [ ] **M1-00 — Freeze decisions and characterize existing behavior**

  - Record the decisions above in the initial draft of `docs/contracts/render-backend-v1.md`.
  - Preserve the dirty snapshot; record baseline status and dependency-based skips without resetting or reformatting anything.
  - Add characterization tests for all three legacy engine values, nominal-Remotion auto-FFmpeg, audio-reactive early selection, current Remotion props/theme/registry behavior, and existing provenance fields.
  - Record the complete source-search inventory from M1-10 so later gates can prove every bypass is removed.
  - Gate: existing targeted rendering, pack, executor, and Hype tests remain green before extraction begins.

- [ ] **M1-01 — Define and freeze the language-neutral contracts**

  - Add backend-neutral contracts under `astrid/core/rendering/` for renderer, planner, finalizer, `RenderRequest`, `SupportReport`, `RenderPlan`, half-open frame windows, `VideoArtifact`, attachments, `RenderResult`, structured failures, and provenance.
  - Add versioned JSON Schemas for request/result/support/plan/finalization and renderer/planner/finalizer manifests.
  - Define frame ranges as `[start_frame, end_frame)`, rational FPS/time base, dimensions, container, codecs, pixel format, duration tolerance, and audio ownership: `rendered`, `passthrough`, or `none`.
  - Require one primary video; preserve named attachments without requiring the default finalizer to interpret them.
  - Use atomic JSON output through `astrid.core.foundation.atomic_io.write_json_atomic` and hashes through `astrid.core.foundation.hash.sha256_file`.
  - Gate: schema examples round-trip through Python DTOs and a raw JSON validator; unknown versions, malformed frame bounds, core-key overwrites, and invalid attachments fail with structured diagnostics.

- [ ] **M1-02 — Add trusted static discovery and registries**

  - Extend [pack.json](/Users/peteromalley/Documents/reigh-workspace/Astrid-oracle/astrid/core/pack/schemas/v1/pack.json), `pack/permissions.py`, `pack/validate.py`, and trust summaries for `extensions.rendering`.
  - Load manifests through `load_manifest_mapping()` only. Never use the legacy executor `runpy` fallback or `PackResolver` for renderer discovery.
  - Build renderer, planner, and finalizer registries over [CapabilityRegistry](/Users/peteromalley/Documents/reigh-workspace/Astrid-oracle/astrid/core/registry/base.py), [discover_pack_metadata](/Users/peteromalley/Documents/reigh-workspace/Astrid-oracle/astrid/core/pack/discovery.py), `AliasResolver`, and `OverrideStore`.
  - Enforce `<pack>.<name>` ownership, path containment, deterministic precedence/conflict reporting, and resolution evidence containing source kind, pack, manifest digest, alias chain, override, and priority.
  - Define trust explicitly: source/project-local/operator-supplied roots are operator-trusted; installed renderers execute only from active revisions with accepted installation trust. Staged/downloaded candidates remain inspectable but non-executable.
  - Register bare `remotion` and `ffmpeg` compatibility aliases programmatically. Register `hybrid` only as a planner translation.
  - Gate: pack-schema, installation, precedence, conflicts, aliases, overrides, hidden/inactive packs, path traversal, and “listing imports nothing” tests all pass.

- [ ] **M1-03 — Implement the command transport and raw-wire fixture pack**

  - Add the synchronous host transport: unique workdir, sanitized environment, no shell, absolute request/result paths, stdout/stderr capture, process-tree cleanup, result parsing, and structured nonzero/missing-result/invalid-result failures.
  - Commit `tests/fixtures/renderer_packs/raw_command/`, containing a trusted test pack whose renderer reads/writes JSON without importing Astrid’s SDK and produces a deterministic two-second video.
  - Exercise the real acknowledged install/trust path or an explicit trusted discovery record; do not silently treat any arbitrary `ASTRID_PACKS_PATH` entry as executable.
  - Add shared text-only and tiny-media requests under a versioned renderer fixture directory.
  - Gate: raw render/support success, unsupported features, bad protocol version, missing output, malformed JSON, nonzero exit, interruption, cleanup, and static no-import discovery all pass.

- [ ] **M1-04 — Extract host-owned asset, workspace, media, audio, and artifact services**

  - Move reusable cache primitives behind a core service, leaving the training `asset_cache` executor as a compatibility wrapper rather than making core rendering depend on a pack executor.
  - Extract `_RangeHTTPRequestHandler`, asset classification, caching, common-root handling, and URL resolution from the render monolith into an invocation-scoped asset service.
  - Bind servers directly to port `0`, restrict served assets, start them inside the managed lifecycle, shut down and join threads on every exit, and avoid fixed props/staging names.
  - Extend [astrid/core/media.py](/Users/peteromalley/Documents/reigh-workspace/Astrid-oracle/astrid/core/media.py) with a strict probe containing codec, pixel format, time base, audio codec/sample rate/channel layout, duration, FPS, and dimensions. Consolidate useful behavior from `video_editing/executors/cut/probe.py`.
  - Validate output existence and containment, symlinks, duration within one frame, dimensions, FPS/time base, video/audio contracts, attachment paths, and hashes before finalization.
  - Gate: local path, cached URL, existing remote URL, Range/CORS, expired URL, cross-project asset, server failure, artifact mismatch, visual-only, passthrough, and attachment tests pass.

- [ ] **M1-05 — Extract and register the Remotion backend**

  - Move project validation, theme resolution, timeline serialization, element-registry generation, effect asset staging, props creation, subprocess invocation, and backend provenance into `astrid/packs/rendering/backends/remotion/`.
  - Register `rendering.remotion` via a static manifest and invoke it through the same command protocol as the fixture.

  - Make props/staging paths invocation-unique, guard shared registry generation, ensure the HTTP server cannot leak before entering cleanup, join its thread, and reject a successful subprocess that did not produce a valid output.
  - Move private-helper tests from `test_render_remotion_registry.py`, `test_asset_cache.py`, `test_url_pipeline_smoke.py`, and Hype tests beside the extracted modules.

- [ ] **M1-06 — Extract and register the FFmpeg backend**

  - Move current media-only validation/rendering and `audio-reactive-colour` into `astrid/packs/rendering/backends/ffmpeg/`.
  - Express audio-reactive rendering and whole-media optimization as request-sensitive FFmpeg support evidence, not facade-level implementation branches.
  - Preserve current supported behavior while fixing fail-open gaps: speed changes, overlapping audio, visual gaps, missing sources, and unsupported track/clip types must be reported before invocation.
  - Return explicit audio ownership instead of requiring third-party visual renderers to synthesize silence. Any legacy silence compatibility belongs to host audio completion policy.
  - Use the shared safe subprocess path and validate every output.
  - Gate: FFmpeg support reports, command construction, stream-copy fast path, visual gaps, audio mixing, audio-reactive markers, failure cleanup, and output/provenance tests pass.

- [ ] **M1-07 — Extract and register the FFmpeg finalizer**

  - Move `_concat_segments()` behind the finalizer contract as `rendering.ffmpeg-finalizer`.
  - Preflight every segment, use the timeline’s canonical dimensions and rational FPS, and reject incompatible duration/video/audio artifacts before assembly.
  - Stream-copy already compatible outputs where practical; otherwise normalize dimensions, FPS/time base, codecs, pixel format, sample rate, and channel layout, recording every transformation.
  - Handle rendered, passthrough, and absent audio without assuming every segment has an audio stream.
  - Preserve attachments outside the finalizer’s interpretation.
  - Gate: single-segment pass-through, mixed compatible/incompatible segments, 24/25/30 and rational FPS, duration errors, missing audio/video, codec mismatch, normalization provenance, and cleanup tests pass. No hard-coded `fps=30` remains.

- [ ] **M1-08 — Introduce generic single-backend routing and provenance v2**

  - Add the legacy compatibility translator as the only place that knows the short names and surprising auto-FFmpeg behavior.

  - Make [render/run.py](/Users/peteromalley/Documents/reigh-workspace/Astrid-oracle/astrid/packs/rendering/executors/render/run.py:1400) a thin executor/CLI adapter over `RenderService`.

## M2 — Renderer developer kit

- [ ] **M2-00 — Enforce the M1 handoff gate**

  - Verify the frozen reference, schemas, raw fixture, built-in registrations, generic service, and conformance suite exist and pass.
  - If the SDK cannot express the wire schema exactly, stop and amend/re-review M1; do not add SDK-only semantics.
  - Gate: raw fixture passes from both a source checkout and an installed wheel.

- [ ] **M2-01 — Add the public Python SDK**

  - Add `astrid/sdk/rendering.py` with frozen JSON-safe `RenderRequest`, `RenderContext`, `SupportReport`, `VideoArtifact`, `RenderResult`, and attachment types.
  - Define author functions as `render(request, context)` and optional `support(request, context)`; `renderer_main()` adapts them to the exact M1 argv/JSON protocol.
  - Add lazy curated exports through `astrid/__init__.py` and `astrid/sdk/dto.py`, following `sdk/generation.py`.
  - Update `docs/contracts/platform-contract.md`, `tests/_sdk_contract.py`, and `tests/test_sdk_public_surface.py`; keep `import astrid` lightweight.
  - Gate: SDK JSON is semantically identical to raw fixtures, imports stay lazy, and public-surface tests pin the additive API.

- [ ] **M2-02 — Complete `RenderContext` conveniences and conformance**

  - Provide allocated output/work paths, descriptor-based path/URL asset access, manifest-declared subprocess checks, redacted logging/progress, synchronous interruption state, probe/hash helpers, and deterministic cleanup.
  - Add `complete_video`, passthrough/mux/no-audio helpers and attachment completion. These must produce the same JSON fields a raw renderer can write manually.
  - Clarify that permission checks gate host services but are not an OS sandbox under pack permissions v1.
  - Add an SDK fixture renderer, request-sensitive support fixture, visual-only passthrough fixture, no-audio fixture, attachment fixture, and intentional failure fixture.
  - Gate: raw and SDK renderers pass the same fixtures; support alternatives, both visual-only modes, and untouched attachments are proven.

- [ ] **M2-03 — Add the exact four-file scaffold**

  - `astrid renderers create acme.example` writes only `pack.yaml`, `renderer.yaml`, `render.py`, and `test_renderer.py`, with no unresolved placeholders.
  - Point `pack.yaml` directly at root `renderer.yaml`; have the test reference packaged, versioned core fixtures so no fifth fixture file is required.
  - Keep generated adapter glue at or below 50 nonblank, non-comment lines before backend-specific logic.
  - Validate qualified ownership, destination collision behavior, executable command paths, and generated documentation comments.
  - Gate: scaffold, static validation, its generated test, and a two-second smoke render succeed in a fresh temporary directory and installed wheel.

- [ ] **M2-04 — Add renderer CLI discovery, validation, and smoke**

  - Add `astrid/core/rendering/cli.py` using `CommandSpec/register_commands`, shared capability display helpers, and `RecoverableArgumentParser`.
  - Insert `_dispatch_renderers` in `gateway/dispatch.py`, update root help, and allow the developer surface without an attached project session.
  - Implement:
    - `list`: static IDs, pack/source, aliases, protocol, permissions, capabilities, conflicts.
    - `inspect`: full static manifest and exact resolution/override evidence.
    - `validate`: static by default; `--conformance` explicitly executes contract fixtures.
    - `smoke`: renders a selected committed fixture through normal `rendering.render` routing and probes the result.
  - Freeze the common `--json` envelope and verb-specific `data` keys. Listing and inspection must remain no-import/no-execution.
  - Gate: help/gateway/session/registration/JSON/error/recovery tests pass for source, installed, conflicting, untrusted, unknown, supported, and unsupported renderers.

- [ ] **M2-05 — Add portable replay bundles and `replay`**

  - On backend failure, always retain a project-run-scoped bundle keyed by request hash. Successful workdirs are removed unless `--keep-workdir` is requested; V1 adds no background TTL or deletion service.
  - Bundle the resolved request, localized timeline/assets, namespaced config, renderer/manifest identity, support result, stdout/stderr, result or partial result, hashes, and exact replay command.
  - Redact credentials, environment secrets, authorization headers, and signed URL query strings. Localize cached remote inputs so the bundle remains self-contained; require secrets to be supplied again through environment variables.
  - `replay` pins the qualified backend and request hash, records old/current implementation digests, and refuses silent re-resolution to another backend.
  - Gate: intentional failure emits a redacted bundle; replay after correcting the fixture succeeds, keeps the request hash/backend selection, and never reruns the editorial pipeline.

- [ ] **M2-06 — Finish renderer-author documentation**

  - Add a golden-path quickstart: create → edit `render.py` → test → validate → smoke → inspect provenance.
  - Separate advanced support probing and custom finalizers from the minimum path.
  - Add protocol examples usable from Python without the SDK and from a non-Python command.
  - Document permissions, selection and override evidence, configuration namespacing, asset representations, output/audio/attachment contracts, cleanup, failure diagnostics, replay/redaction, and compatibility aliases.
  - State prominently that async submit/status/cancel/resume, production remote rendering, remote upload infrastructure, and layer compositing are deferred.
  - Extend `tests/verify_docs_commands.sh` to cover renderer docs.
  - Gate: all documented commands execute against temporary fixtures and contain no stale direct-module paths.

- [ ] **M2-07 — Epic-wide verification and source freeze**

  - Run the complete M1/M2 contract matrix for raw-wire and SDK renderers, built-ins, strict qualified IDs, all legacy names, mixed planning, audio modes, attachments, failure/replay, aliases, overrides, and installed discovery.
  - Assert planner/dispatcher/service source contains no concrete Remotion/FFmpeg branch. Built-in names may appear only in registrations, implementations, legacy translation, and intentional tests/docs.
  - Verify every successful path produces one valid sidecar and every failed backend produces a replay bundle.
  - Run targeted tests, full pytest, explicit parity/real-render lanes, `make check`, `make ci`, wheel smoke, and Remotion typecheck.
  - Final review each task group independently: contract/discovery, built-in extraction, generic routing/hybrid, caller migration/verification, SDK/scaffold, CLI/replay/docs.

# 2. Additional areas to explore

These are bounded execution-time investigations, not invitations to reopen the locked architecture.

| Area | Concrete targets | What to establish |
|---|---|---|
| Pack discovery order | `astrid/core/pack/discovery.py`, `loader.py`, `store.py` | Exact source/local/extra/env/installed precedence and how active installed revisions are represented in provenance. |
| Trust boundary | `install_local.py`, `install_trust.py`, `validate.py` | Add an explicit static trust decision without pretending pack permissions are sandbox enforcement. |
| Manifest schema parity | `pack/schemas/v1/pack.json`, `pack/permissions.py`, `_common.py` | Update both the JSON Schema and manual normalizer; extend alias kinds and component inventory consistently. |
| Registry semantics | `registry/base.py`, `execution/executor/registry.py`, `pack/alias_resolver.py`, `pack/override.py` | Preserve conflict, priority, alias-chain, and override order; avoid the current SDK/default-registry override inconsistency. |
| Project-run ownership | `contracts/capability_runner.py`, `execution/executor/runner.py`, `project/run.py` | Confirm the public caller API creates one facade run while backend invocation creates none. |
| Facade command compatibility | `executor.yaml`, `runner.py::_normalize_render_command_compat`, `pack/entrypoint.py` | Preserve theme argument ordering and canonical entrypoint behavior while thinning `run.py`. |
| Renderer output enforcement | `executor/runner.py::resolve_declared_output_paths`, result-manifest helpers | The runner currently ignores missing declared outputs; decide whether strictness remains renderer-local or becomes an opt-in runner contract. |
| Provenance consumers | `_delete_previous_render_outputs_for_timeline`, hybrid ingestion, iteration finalization, rendering tests/docs | Confirm there are no out-of-repository consumers before later removing v1 mirrors. |
| Canonical canvas/FPS | `timeline/model.py`, timeline validators, theme resolution, Remotion `Root.tsx` | Reconcile `theme_overrides` with merged theme canvas and preserve rational FPS without redesigning timeline schema. |
| Hybrid planner | `_complex_clip_windows`, `_hybrid_segments`, handle math | Characterize overlaps, speed changes, audio overlaps, transitions, opacity, and rounding before porting. |
| FFmpeg behavior | `_validate_ffmpeg_media_timeline`, `_render_ffmpeg_media`, audio-reactive module | Separate compatibility-worthy behavior from fail-open bugs such as ignored visual gaps and incomplete support detection. |
| Media normalization | `astrid/core/media.py`, `video_editing/executors/cut/probe.py`, `_concat_segments` | Define exact codec/time-base/audio compatibility needed for pass-through versus normalization. |
| Audio ownership | FFmpeg filters, audio render tests, timeline audio model | Decide when legacy silence remains required and prove third-party visual renderers can return passthrough or none. |
| Asset cache boundary | `training/executors/asset_cache/run.py`, render asset helpers | Rehome shared cache primitives without forcing unrelated pack callers to migrate in the same commit. |
| Local serving | `_RangeHTTPRequestHandler`, `_server_root_for`, related tests | Address root exposure, bind races, thread joining, signed URL expiry, and concurrent renders. |
| Remotion lifecycle | render setup/staging helpers, `scripts/gen_effect_registry.py`, Remotion package scripts | Protect shared generated registries, unique props/staging paths, subprocess process groups, and zero-exit/no-output cases. |
| Effect/theme parity | `test_render_remotion_registry.py`, `tests/golden/hype`, rendering skill | Preserve registry invalidation, source-pack lineage, active theme, and the actual `TimelineComposition` default. |
| Runtime callsites | iteration, cut/resume, Hype steps, human notes | Ensure facade executor overrides and renderer overrides affect every path and remove cached direct-module resolution. |
| Test coupling | private imports in render tests, asset cache tests, Hype tests | Move tests to extracted boundaries while retaining a small facade contract suite. |
| Fixture authority | `tests/fixtures/sprint08`, `tests/fixtures/reshape/hype_regression`, `tests/golden/hype` | Replace the empty/input-hash parity story with versioned semantic fixtures and real media probes. |
| CLI insertion | `gateway/dispatch.py`, `gateway/help.py`, `gateway/__init__.py`, CLI registration tests | Confirm all six verbs are unbound developer operations and do not collide with lifecycle `--engine task|arnold`. |
| `--engine` surfaces | facade parser, executor manifest, cut `--renderer`, Blender’s unrelated `--engine` | Widen only timeline-renderer validation and avoid global choice-registry changes that affect unrelated engines. |
| SDK conventions | `astrid/__init__.py`, `sdk/generation.py`, `sdk/results.py`, public-surface tests | Keep imports lazy, DTOs frozen/JSON-safe, and top-level exports SemVer-accounted. |
| Scaffold/package data | pack scaffold utilities, `pyproject.toml`, wheel smoke script | Guarantee exactly four generated files while packaging shared schemas, fixtures, and templates. |
| Error envelopes | `core/contracts/errors.py`, `exec_error.py`, subprocess helpers | Map backend, protocol, unsupported, artifact, and finalizer failures into existing Astrid envelopes and stable symbolic codes. |
| Replay security | env filtering, URL cache metadata, subprocess logs | Redact credentials and signed URLs from requests, commands, logs, and partial results without making replay unusable. |
| CI lanes | workflow files, `docs/guides/ci-lanes.md`, Remotion scripts | Identify which real-render dependencies are guaranteed in blocking CI and make every optional skip explicit. |
| Terminology collision | `astrid/core/modalities`, iteration’s `renderers` flags | Keep modality render hints distinct from timeline backend IDs in API names and documentation. |
| Documentation drift | rendering skill/STAGE, `_core` skill, `render-adapter.md`, bridge contract | Remove stale direct-run instructions and use the actual pack-doc paths under `docs/packs/`. |

# 3. Open questions / potential issues for execution

The spec’s ten open questions are resolved above. The remaining items are implementation hazards or existing defects to close through tests.

- Plain FFmpeg, nominal-Remotion→FFmpeg, and all-FFmpeg hybrid currently return without the manifest-declared provenance sidecar.

- `cut --render` and `cut resume --render` import a nonexistent sibling `render` module. The current smoke tests commonly skip before reaching that path.

- Cut’s `--renderer` accepts only `remotion`, is written to metadata, and is not passed to rendering.

- `iteration_video` moves `hype.mp4` to `iteration.mp4` but leaves the sidecar behind with a stale `output` value.

- Legacy Hype and `human_notes --apply` resolve the executor module and spawn it directly. This bypasses normal runner output/isolation/ledger behavior, and `executor_argv()` caches resolution globally, which can make overrides stale.

- Hybrid’s current pure-FFmpeg path emits no hybrid provenance; mixed provenance omits FFmpeg segments and records temporary Remotion paths that are immediately deleted.

- Hybrid planning misses some FFmpeg-invalid features, including speed changes and overlapping audio, then recursively invokes the monolithic `render()`.

- `_concat_segments()` assumes audio on every segment, does no probe, always re-encodes, and hard-codes 30 FPS, H.264/AAC, stereo 44.1 kHz.

- Python hybrid canvas resolution ignores the merged theme canvas used by Remotion, so theme-defined dimensions/FPS can disagree between planning and rendering.

- FFmpeg currently synthesizes silent audio. Preserve output compatibility only where the legacy policy requires it; do not make silence a renderer contract requirement.

- The current `MediaProbe` is best-effort and lacks codecs, pixel format, time base, and audio metadata. It is insufficient for the finalizer contract.

- A zero-exit Remotion subprocess is treated as success even if it produces no output.

- Remotion serving can leak if failure happens before the current `try/finally`; the thread is not joined, port discovery is racy, and fixed props/staging paths collide across concurrent renders.

- Element registry generation mutates shared generated files and active-theme pointers without locking or atomic replacement.

- The existing Sprint 08 parity test does not render anything. Its fixture directory contains only a README and its assertion hashes timeline JSON.

- Direct module commands documented in skills/STAGE files are normally rejected by `guard_canonical_entrypoint()`. They must be removed or explicitly labeled internal debugging commands.

- The rendering skill names `HypeComposition`, while the implementation uses `TimelineComposition`.

- `renderer.yaml` command-path semantics must be frozen: pack-root working directory, no shell, executable lookup through `PATH`, pack-contained relative script paths, and absolute request/result paths.

- Pack permissions v1 are disclosure-only. “Permission-aware subprocess” can reject missing declarations and restrict host helpers, but it cannot honestly promise isolation from filesystem or network access.

- The rendering pack currently has pack-wide network permission for unrelated rendering features. Renderer inspection should show both pack permissions and the renderer’s required subset to avoid implying Remotion or FFmpeg needs every permission.

- Source, project-local, extra-root, environment-root, and installed packs do not currently carry a uniform `trusted` field. Execution must derive and record trust explicitly rather than equating parsing with authorization.

- Renderer aliases and overrides must use the same resolution order from CLI, SDK, facade, planner, and replay. The existing default executor registry’s omission of the override store is a precedent to avoid.

- A renderer override that resolves back to the facade or creates an alias cycle must fail before invocation.

- Result and attachment paths must be contained within the allocated workspace until atomic publication; reject traversal, malicious symlinks, duplicate attachment names, and attempts to overwrite request inputs.

- Stream-copy compatibility requires more than matching filename extensions: time base, codec/profile/level, pixel format, dimensions, audio layout, and sometimes codec extradata matter.

- Remote and signed URLs complicate hashes and replay. Materialize a local hashed copy before invocation whenever possible and never persist credentials or expiring query strings.

- Failure cleanup and replay retention pull in opposite directions. The service needs separate “publishable artifacts,” “replay bundle,” and disposable work areas so retaining diagnostics does not expose arbitrary temporary files.

- V1 exposes only read-only cooperative interruption state. It must not accidentally grow submit/status/cancel job semantics or imply resumable remote execution.

- `astrid renderers` should be unbound from project sessions, but `smoke` still needs deterministic temporary output ownership and must not silently attach to or mutate the current project.

- Public SDK additions change the exact `import astrid` contract. Missing top-level export and wheel-package tests would make a working internal SDK still fail the repository’s public-surface rules.

- The exact four-file scaffold conflicts with copying fixtures into each generated pack. Versioned packaged fixtures must therefore be present in source and wheels.

- Renderer manifests, JSON Schemas, scaffold templates, and smoke fixtures are new package data. The current wheel smoke test does not verify any of them.

- Real FFmpeg and Remotion gates depend on external binaries/packages. CI must distinguish an intentional documented local skip from a supposedly passing lane that exercised no renderer.

- The two paths named in the brief have moved: the actual files are `docs/packs/creating-packs.md` and `docs/packs/aliases-vs-forks-vs-overrides.md`.

There is no user decision blocking execution. The first implementation unit should be M1-00/M1-01; extraction should not begin until the decisions, schemas, compatibility matrix, and baseline characterization tests are committed and reviewed.
tokens used
365,333
# 1. Entire-project tasklist

No files were changed during this planning pass. The 18 locked decisions remain unchanged. The following closes all ten open questions and decomposes M1 and M2 into independently reviewable, test-gated units.

## Resolved architecture decisions

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| 1 | Use a rendering-specific pack extension, backed by the existing pack kernel—not ordinary child executors. `pack.yaml` gains an optional `extensions.rendering` section containing pack-relative `renderer.yaml`, planner, and finalizer manifests. Reuse `discover_pack_metadata`, `CapabilityRegistry`, `AliasResolver`, and `OverrideStore`; do not create another pack loader. |
| 2 | `rendering.render` owns the sole executor/project run. It calls backend commands directly through the render host, so backends do not create nested runs, ledgers, task gates, or CLI recursion. External callers invoke the `rendering.render` capability; only its facade calls the internal `RenderService`. |
| 3 | Minimal renderer metadata is: qualified `id`, manifest/protocol version, argv `command`, supported operations, coarse `clip_types`, `track_types`, feature tokens, full-timeline/window support, output-profile hints, required binaries, and the subset of pack permissions it needs. Missing capabilities fail closed. Optional `support` is the authoritative request-sensitive answer. |
| 4 | The canonical request carries `backend_config: { "<qualified-id>": {...} }`. Legacy typed executor flags may translate into that map at the facade, but backend-specific fields never enter the core request schema. Only the selected backend receives its map. |
| 5 | Provenance v2 keeps top-level compatibility projections for `schema_version`, `engine`, `output`, `timeline`, and `assets_registry`. `engine` records the legacy request; new requested/resolved fields are authoritative. Remotion-only fields move under a backend-owned `rendering.remotion` fragment. Deprecated v1 Remotion and segment fields are mirrored for one compatibility window, then removed only after a consumer audit. |
| 6 | `ffmpeg` maps to strict `rendering.ffmpeg`; `remotion` retains the current legacy policy—FFmpeg for eligible media/audio-specialized timelines, otherwise `rendering.remotion`; `hybrid` maps to `rendering.legacy_hybrid`, never a renderer ID. Qualified `rendering.remotion` and `rendering.ffmpeg` are strict. An auto-routing warning plus provenance reason makes legacy `remotion` behavior visible; a later release can move it to `planner=auto`. |
| 7 | The current Sprint 08 “parity” fixture is not authoritative: it contains no render fixtures and hashes input JSON. Gate extraction on semantic command/props/provenance and `ffprobe` parity using the existing Remotion registry/theme tests, audio-reactive fixture, Hype merged-props golden, and `reshape/hype_regression`; add committed 2-second fixtures. Do not require byte-identical MP4s. |
| 8 | The Python author API is functional, without inheritance: `render(request, context)` plus optional `support(request, context)`, registered through an SDK `renderer_main(...)` helper. `RenderContext` supplies resolved assets, allocated paths, declared-permission checks, subprocess/log/progress helpers, read-only synchronous interruption state, probing, hashes, audio completion, attachments, and cleanup. The wire protocol—not the SDK—defines semantics. |
| 9 | Every asset uses one descriptor with original reference, hash/metadata, and whichever of `local_path`, invocation-scoped `local_url`, or existing `remote_url` are available. The host materializes local/cache copies and starts Range serving only when requested. Remote adapters upload themselves under declared permissions; V1 adds no general remote-upload service. |
| 10 | The public author surface is `astrid renderers create/list/inspect/validate/smoke/replay`. `list` and `inspect` are static. `validate` is static unless explicit conformance execution is requested; `smoke` and `replay` execute code. All return a common JSON envelope: `schema_version`, `command`, `ok`, `data`, and `errors`, with backend-qualified symbolic errors and `recovery_command`. Expected failures retain Astrid’s exit 2 convention; escaped bugs return 1; interruption returns 130. Pack installation remains under `astrid packs`. A thin kind-aware override administration path should reuse `OverrideStore`, not copy executor override logic. |

The concrete extension shape to freeze in M1 is:

```yaml
extensions:
  rendering:
    renderers:
      - renderer.yaml
    planners: []
    finalizers: []
```

Manifest paths are pack-relative, must remain inside the pack, and are parsed statically. Commands run with `shell=False`, with the pack root as `cwd`. The V1 invocation shape is:

```text
<manifest command...> render|support|plan|finalize \
  --request <absolute-request.json> \
  --result <absolute-result.json>
```

The result file is authoritative; stdout and stderr are captured as logs.

## M2 — Renderer developer kit

- [ ] **M2-00 — Enforce the M1 handoff gate**

  - Add `complete_video`, passthrough/mux/no-audio helpers and attachment completion. These must produce the same JSON fields a raw renderer can write manually.

  - `astrid renderers create acme.example` writes only `pack.yaml`, `renderer.yaml`, `render.py`, and `test_renderer.py`, with no unresolved placeholders.
  - Point `pack.yaml` directly at root `renderer.yaml`; have the test reference packaged, versioned core fixtures so no fifth fixture file is required.

  - Gate: scaffold, static validation, its generated test, and a two-second smoke render succeed in a fresh temporary directory and installed wheel.

- [ ] **M2-04 — Add renderer CLI discovery, validation, and smoke**

  - Add `astrid/core/rendering/cli.py` using `CommandSpec/register_commands`, shared capability display helpers, and `RecoverableArgumentParser`.
  - Insert `_dispatch_renderers` in `gateway/dispatch.py`, update root help, and allow the developer surface without an attached project session.
  - Implement:
    - `list`: static IDs, pack/source, aliases, protocol, permissions, capabilities, conflicts.
    - `inspect`: full static manifest and exact resolution/override evidence.
    - `validate`: static by default; `--conformance` explicitly executes contract fixtures.
    - `smoke`: renders a selected committed fixture through normal `rendering.render` routing and probes the result.
  - Freeze the common `--json` envelope and verb-specific `data` keys. Listing and inspection must remain no-import/no-execution.
  - Gate: help/gateway/session/registration/JSON/error/recovery tests pass for source, installed, conflicting, untrusted, unknown, supported, and unsupported renderers.

- [ ] **M2-05 — Add portable replay bundles and `replay`**

  - On backend failure, always retain a project-run-scoped bundle keyed by request hash. Successful workdirs are removed unless `--keep-workdir` is requested; V1 adds no background TTL or deletion service.
  - Bundle the resolved request, localized timeline/assets, namespaced config, renderer/manifest identity, support result, stdout/stderr, result or partial result, hashes, and exact replay command.
  - Redact credentials, environment secrets, authorization headers, and signed URL query strings. Localize cached remote inputs so the bundle remains self-contained; require secrets to be supplied again through environment variables.
  - `replay` pins the qualified backend and request hash, records old/current implementation digests, and refuses silent re-resolution to another backend.
  - Gate: intentional failure emits a redacted bundle; replay after correcting the fixture succeeds, keeps the request hash/backend selection, and never reruns the editorial pipeline.

- [ ] **M2-06 — Finish renderer-author documentation**

  - Add a golden-path quickstart: create → edit `render.py` → test → validate → smoke → inspect provenance.
  - Separate advanced support probing and custom finalizers from the minimum path.
  - Add protocol examples usable from Python without the SDK and from a non-Python command.
  - Document permissions, selection and override evidence, configuration namespacing, asset representations, output/audio/attachment contracts, cleanup, failure diagnostics, replay/redaction, and compatibility aliases.
  - State prominently that async submit/status/cancel/resume, production remote rendering, remote upload infrastructure, and layer compositing are deferred.
  - Extend `tests/verify_docs_commands.sh` to cover renderer docs.
  - Gate: all documented commands execute against temporary fixtures and contain no stale direct-module paths.

- [ ] **M2-07 — Epic-wide verification and source freeze**

  - Run the complete M1/M2 contract matrix for raw-wire and SDK renderers, built-ins, strict qualified IDs, all legacy names, mixed planning, audio modes, attachments, failure/replay, aliases, overrides, and installed discovery.
  - Assert planner/dispatcher/service source contains no concrete Remotion/FFmpeg branch. Built-in names may appear only in registrations, implementations, legacy translation, and intentional tests/docs.
  - Verify every successful path produces one valid sidecar and every failed backend produces a replay bundle.
  - Run targeted tests, full pytest, explicit parity/real-render lanes, `make check`, `make ci`, wheel smoke, and Remotion typecheck.
  - Final review each task group independently: contract/discovery, built-in extraction, generic routing/hybrid, caller migration/verification, SDK/scaffold, CLI/replay/docs.

# 2. Additional areas to explore

These are bounded execution-time investigations, not invitations to reopen the locked architecture.

| Area | Concrete targets | What to establish |
|---|---|---|
| Pack discovery order | `astrid/core/pack/discovery.py`, `loader.py`, `store.py` | Exact source/local/extra/env/installed precedence and how active installed revisions are represented in provenance. |
| Trust boundary | `install_local.py`, `install_trust.py`, `validate.py` | Add an explicit static trust decision without pretending pack permissions are sandbox enforcement. |
| Manifest schema parity | `pack/schemas/v1/pack.json`, `pack/permissions.py`, `_common.py` | Update both the JSON Schema and manual normalizer; extend alias kinds and component inventory consistently. |
| Registry semantics | `registry/base.py`, `execution/executor/registry.py`, `pack/alias_resolver.py`, `pack/override.py` | Preserve conflict, priority, alias-chain, and override order; avoid the current SDK/default-registry override inconsistency. |
| Project-run ownership | `contracts/capability_runner.py`, `execution/executor/runner.py`, `project/run.py` | Confirm the public caller API creates one facade run while backend invocation creates none. |
| Facade command compatibility | `executor.yaml`, `runner.py::_normalize_render_command_compat`, `pack/entrypoint.py` | Preserve theme argument ordering and canonical entrypoint behavior while thinning `run.py`. |
| Renderer output enforcement | `executor/runner.py::resolve_declared_output_paths`, result-manifest helpers | The runner currently ignores missing declared outputs; decide whether strictness remains renderer-local or becomes an opt-in runner contract. |
| Provenance consumers | `_delete_previous_render_outputs_for_timeline`, hybrid ingestion, iteration finalization, rendering tests/docs | Confirm there are no out-of-repository consumers before later removing v1 mirrors. |
| Canonical canvas/FPS | `timeline/model.py`, timeline validators, theme resolution, Remotion `Root.tsx` | Reconcile `theme_overrides` with merged theme canvas and preserve rational FPS without redesigning timeline schema. |
| Hybrid planner | `_complex_clip_windows`, `_hybrid_segments`, handle math | Characterize overlaps, speed changes, audio overlaps, transitions, opacity, and rounding before porting. |
| FFmpeg behavior | `_validate_ffmpeg_media_timeline`, `_render_ffmpeg_media`, audio-reactive module | Separate compatibility-worthy behavior from fail-open bugs such as ignored visual gaps and incomplete support detection. |
| Media normalization | `astrid/core/media.py`, `video_editing/executors/cut/probe.py`, `_concat_segments` | Define exact codec/time-base/audio compatibility needed for pass-through versus normalization. |
| Audio ownership | FFmpeg filters, audio render tests, timeline audio model | Decide when legacy silence remains required and prove third-party visual renderers can return passthrough or none. |
| Asset cache boundary | `training/executors/asset_cache/run.py`, render asset helpers | Rehome shared cache primitives without forcing unrelated pack callers to migrate in the same commit. |
| Local serving | `_RangeHTTPRequestHandler`, `_server_root_for`, related tests | Address root exposure, bind races, thread joining, signed URL expiry, and concurrent renders. |
| Remotion lifecycle | render setup/staging helpers, `scripts/gen_effect_registry.py`, Remotion package scripts | Protect shared generated registries, unique props/staging paths, subprocess process groups, and zero-exit/no-output cases. |
| Effect/theme parity | `test_render_remotion_registry.py`, `tests/golden/hype`, rendering skill | Preserve registry invalidation, source-pack lineage, active theme, and the actual `TimelineComposition` default. |
| Runtime callsites | iteration, cut/resume, Hype steps, human notes | Ensure facade executor overrides and renderer overrides affect every path and remove cached direct-module resolution. |
| Test coupling | private imports in render tests, asset cache tests, Hype tests | Move tests to extracted boundaries while retaining a small facade contract suite. |
| Fixture authority | `tests/fixtures/sprint08`, `tests/fixtures/reshape/hype_regression`, `tests/golden/hype` | Replace the empty/input-hash parity story with versioned semantic fixtures and real media probes. |
| CLI insertion | `gateway/dispatch.py`, `gateway/help.py`, `gateway/__init__.py`, CLI registration tests | Confirm all six verbs are unbound developer operations and do not collide with lifecycle `--engine task|arnold`. |
| `--engine` surfaces | facade parser, executor manifest, cut `--renderer`, Blender’s unrelated `--engine` | Widen only timeline-renderer validation and avoid global choice-registry changes that affect unrelated engines. |
| SDK conventions | `astrid/__init__.py`, `sdk/generation.py`, `sdk/results.py`, public-surface tests | Keep imports lazy, DTOs frozen/JSON-safe, and top-level exports SemVer-accounted. |
| Scaffold/package data | pack scaffold utilities, `pyproject.toml`, wheel smoke script | Guarantee exactly four generated files while packaging shared schemas, fixtures, and templates. |
| Error envelopes | `core/contracts/errors.py`, `exec_error.py`, subprocess helpers | Map backend, protocol, unsupported, artifact, and finalizer failures into existing Astrid envelopes and stable symbolic codes. |
| Replay security | env filtering, URL cache metadata, subprocess logs | Redact credentials and signed URLs from requests, commands, logs, and partial results without making replay unusable. |
| CI lanes | workflow files, `docs/guides/ci-lanes.md`, Remotion scripts | Identify which real-render dependencies are guaranteed in blocking CI and make every optional skip explicit. |
| Terminology collision | `astrid/core/modalities`, iteration’s `renderers` flags | Keep modality render hints distinct from timeline backend IDs in API names and documentation. |
| Documentation drift | rendering skill/STAGE, `_core` skill, `render-adapter.md`, bridge contract | Remove stale direct-run instructions and use the actual pack-doc paths under `docs/packs/`. |

# 3. Open questions / potential issues for execution

The spec’s ten open questions are resolved above. The remaining items are implementation hazards or existing defects to close through tests.

- Plain FFmpeg, nominal-Remotion→FFmpeg, and all-FFmpeg hybrid currently return without the manifest-declared provenance sidecar.

- `cut --render` and `cut resume --render` import a nonexistent sibling `render` module. The current smoke tests commonly skip before reaching that path.

- Cut’s `--renderer` accepts only `remotion`, is written to metadata, and is not passed to rendering.

- `iteration_video` moves `hype.mp4` to `iteration.mp4` but leaves the sidecar behind with a stale `output` value.

- Legacy Hype and `human_notes --apply` resolve the executor module and spawn it directly. This bypasses normal runner output/isolation/ledger behavior, and `executor_argv()` caches resolution globally, which can make overrides stale.

- Hybrid’s current pure-FFmpeg path emits no hybrid provenance; mixed provenance omits FFmpeg segments and records temporary Remotion paths that are immediately deleted.

- Hybrid planning misses some FFmpeg-invalid features, including speed changes and overlapping audio, then recursively invokes the monolithic `render()`.

- `_concat_segments()` assumes audio on every segment, does no probe, always re-encodes, and hard-codes 30 FPS, H.264/AAC, stereo 44.1 kHz.

- Python hybrid canvas resolution ignores the merged theme canvas used by Remotion, so theme-defined dimensions/FPS can disagree between planning and rendering.

- FFmpeg currently synthesizes silent audio. Preserve output compatibility only where the legacy policy requires it; do not make silence a renderer contract requirement.

- The current `MediaProbe` is best-effort and lacks codecs, pixel format, time base, and audio metadata. It is insufficient for the finalizer contract.

- A zero-exit Remotion subprocess is treated as success even if it produces no output.

- Remotion serving can leak if failure happens before the current `try/finally`; the thread is not joined, port discovery is racy, and fixed props/staging paths collide across concurrent renders.

- Element registry generation mutates shared generated files and active-theme pointers without locking or atomic replacement.

- The existing Sprint 08 parity test does not render anything. Its fixture directory contains only a README and its assertion hashes timeline JSON.

- Direct module commands documented in skills/STAGE files are normally rejected by `guard_canonical_entrypoint()`. They must be removed or explicitly labeled internal debugging commands.

- The rendering skill names `HypeComposition`, while the implementation uses `TimelineComposition`.

- `renderer.yaml` command-path semantics must be frozen: pack-root working directory, no shell, executable lookup through `PATH`, pack-contained relative script paths, and absolute request/result paths.

- Pack permissions v1 are disclosure-only. “Permission-aware subprocess” can reject missing declarations and restrict host helpers, but it cannot honestly promise isolation from filesystem or network access.

- The rendering pack currently has pack-wide network permission for unrelated rendering features. Renderer inspection should show both pack permissions and the renderer’s required subset to avoid implying Remotion or FFmpeg needs every permission.

- Source, project-local, extra-root, environment-root, and installed packs do not currently carry a uniform `trusted` field. Execution must derive and record trust explicitly rather than equating parsing with authorization.

- Renderer aliases and overrides must use the same resolution order from CLI, SDK, facade, planner, and replay. The existing default executor registry’s omission of the override store is a precedent to avoid.

- A renderer override that resolves back to the facade or creates an alias cycle must fail before invocation.

- Result and attachment paths must be contained within the allocated workspace until atomic publication; reject traversal, malicious symlinks, duplicate attachment names, and attempts to overwrite request inputs.

- Stream-copy compatibility requires more than matching filename extensions: time base, codec/profile/level, pixel format, dimensions, audio layout, and sometimes codec extradata matter.

- Remote and signed URLs complicate hashes and replay. Materialize a local hashed copy before invocation whenever possible and never persist credentials or expiring query strings.

- Failure cleanup and replay retention pull in opposite directions. The service needs separate “publishable artifacts,” “replay bundle,” and disposable work areas so retaining diagnostics does not expose arbitrary temporary files.

- V1 exposes only read-only cooperative interruption state. It must not accidentally grow submit/status/cancel job semantics or imply resumable remote execution.

- `astrid renderers` should be unbound from project sessions, but `smoke` still needs deterministic temporary output ownership and must not silently attach to or mutate the current project.

- Public SDK additions change the exact `import astrid` contract. Missing top-level export and wheel-package tests would make a working internal SDK still fail the repository’s public-surface rules.

- The exact four-file scaffold conflicts with copying fixtures into each generated pack. Versioned packaged fixtures must therefore be present in source and wheels.

- Renderer manifests, JSON Schemas, scaffold templates, and smoke fixtures are new package data. The current wheel smoke test does not verify any of them.

- Real FFmpeg and Remotion gates depend on external binaries/packages. CI must distinguish an intentional documented local skip from a supposedly passing lane that exercised no renderer.

- The two paths named in the brief have moved: the actual files are `docs/packs/creating-packs.md` and `docs/packs/aliases-vs-forks-vs-overrides.md`.

There is no user decision blocking execution. The first implementation unit should be M1-00/M1-01; extraction should not begin until the decisions, schemas, compatibility matrix, and baseline characterization tests are committed and reviewed.
